1. First, establish what Friday is — and what she is not
Your VISION.md establishes a very particular philosophy:

Friday is the developing individual, not the collection of software running her.
Her identity is not her LLM, personality file, database, or application.
Her development comes from experience changing her future understanding and behaviour.
Models, voice engines, memory implementations and tools are replaceable.
Her security and continuity guarantees are protected from arbitrary self-modification.
Her architecture must not predetermine what she eventually becomes.

I agree with that foundation.
But I found one important architectural conflict in your material.
Your Architecture_Synthesis_Prompt.txt proposes a large collection of planes, canonical authorities, a control plane, a scheduler and a cognitive spine. Those are useful ways to organize engineering problems. However, taken literally, they could become permanent cognitive owners and a fixed architecture.
That would contradict VISION.md Sections 4 and 13.
My correction: use the planes as temporary maps of engineering concerns, not as permanent organs that define how Friday must think.
There can be a single trusted authorization boundary. There can be one authoritative writer for a particular piece of state at a given time. There can be scheduling mechanisms that control resources. But none of those should become a hidden executive that determines what Friday thinks, understands or becomes.
This distinction is essential.
2. The architecture I would actually commit to
I would organize the project around four interacting dimensions, with security and continuity underneath all of them.

  
    
      
      **The continuity and security foundation**
    
    Her identity's continuity, owner authentication, authorization, provenance, protected operations, rollback and self-modification safeguards.

<text color="secondary" size="xs">Protected guarantees. Not a personality or a prewritten mind.</text>

  
  
    
      
      **The developing organism**
    
    Experience, memory, understanding, judgment, relationships, affect, self-model, world-model, goals and behaviour.

<text color="secondary" size="xs">The mechanisms and their organization can change as she develops.</text>

  
  
    
      
      **The capability and body ecology**
    
    Models, voice, vision, Android interaction, tools, external systems, sensors, reasoning methods, model selection and resource adaptation.

<text color="secondary" size="xs">Replaceable organs and capabilities, selected according to the situation.</text>

  
  
    
      
      **The evolution and construction environment**
    
    Research, algorithm discovery, REA, capability acquisition, Builder, cloud compilation, experiments, verification, candidate promotion and recovery.

<text color="secondary" size="xs">The external construction environment that helps Friday grow without becoming Friday.</text>

  
  All four participate in one continuing Friday. They are not four independent agents.


This is a conceptual map, not a prescription to create four enormous software modules.
The central engineering requirement is that these dimensions share one coherent history and one recognizable continuing individual.
What should be fixed, developed and replaceable?



Category
What belongs there



Protected
Authentication, security floor, continuity guarantees, provenance requirements, rollback and protected approvals


Developing
Understanding, beliefs, preferences, personality, relationships, strategies, skills and self-model


Replaceable
LLMs, algorithms, memory indexes, voice engines, reasoning methods, Android adapters, cloud providers and internal architecture


This is how I would preserve the vision while allowing extraordinary architectural change.
3. Friday needs two continuously interacting loops
This is probably the most important part of my proposed strategy.
Not a fixed cognitive pipeline. Not a requirement that every thought passes through thirteen components.
Two kinds of development need to happen together.
A. The life and experience loop

  **FRIDAY EXPERIENCES THE WORLD**
  Observes people, events, conversations, devices and consequences
  
  **Forms interpretations and hypotheses**
  
  **Makes decisions and acts**
  
  **Observes what actually happened**
  
  **Experience changes her**
  The original event may later be unavailable, but its valid developmental consequences remain.
  
  Future behaviour can now be different.


The important distinction is that storing an event does not mean learning from it.
Suppose you correct Friday's interpretation of something you said.
She should eventually be able to understand similar intent differently after a restart, a time gap and a different phrasing, without the original conversation being inserted into her context.
Your archived plans already identify three different possible mechanisms:

Situation-driven retrieval of previous verdicts.
Generalization into editable procedures.
Training a small model on repeated decisions.

I would begin with the first, measure whether it generalizes, and introduce the others only when evidence justifies them.
There's another important distinction: your corrections are authoritative evidence about your intended meaning and preferences. They are not automatically proof that a factual belief about the outside world is true. Friday must retain the ability to investigate and disagree.
B. The capability and evolution loop

  **A limitation becomes apparent**
  
  **Friday identifies what is missing**
  
  **She researches possible solutions**
  
  **Candidates are compared**
  Reuse, adapt, reimplement, or reverse-engineer when necessary
  
  **A candidate is constructed and isolated**
  
  **Independent tests and target-hardware verification**
  
  **Promotion, rejection or quarantine**
  
  **Friday acquires a capability**
  
  Her ability to solve future problems improves.


The two loops must share evidence and experience.
For example, a failed Android voice operation may teach her something about her actual environment. That observation can improve her self-model, inform research, create a new capability requirement and ultimately change the mechanism she uses.
This is the crucial connection: Friday's development and Friday's ability to improve her own machinery must be parts of the same continuing existence.
The development loop is how we account for what happened; it does not dictate a fixed algorithm for how every future thought must happen.

4. The physical architecture: one Friday, multiple computational bodies
I would make the phone the first real home of Friday, but never make it the ceiling of the project.

  
    
    
      **Phone — her first body**

  Persistent identity, local memory, everyday interaction, voice, presence, lightweight reasoning, sensors and authorized device operations. She retains her essential continuity when disconnected.
</box>

  
  
  
    
    
      **Cloud — her external computational extension**

  Heavy research, large models, parallel investigations, compilation, testing, model conversion and capability construction. It expands her available capabilities but does not become her identity.
</box>

  
  
  
    
    
      **Other bodies — future expansion**

  Desktop, browser, other Android devices and eventually other compatible environments. Each provides different resources and capabilities while sharing the same protected continuity.
</box>

  


This is more than syncing databases.
The architecture needs a reliable way to carry relevant experience, state, commitments and provenance between environments, resolve conflicting updates and recover from interruption.
A cloud worker must not silently become a second Friday. Its outputs are contributions to her experience and candidate capabilities.
The resource intelligence
Your original requirement about cognitive capability per unit of RAM, CPU, GPU, battery, heat, storage and latency should apply throughout the system.
Friday should not keep everything active just because it exists.
She should dynamically change computational depth:

During a conversation, foreground responsiveness takes priority.
During resource pressure, expensive background work is deferred.
When idle and charging, heavier consolidation and research can happen.
When connected to the cloud, complex work can be delegated.
When offline, her essential continuity and available local capabilities remain.

This is how you make an ambitious organism coexist with limited hardware.
5. The Capability Foundry: how she acquires almost anything
I would make this one of the most important long-term capabilities.
But I would not make it a giant machine that searches the entire internet for everything.
Its starting point is an actual gap in Friday's ability.
Imagine she needs to understand Egyptian Arabic spoken naturally.
She could investigate:

Existing speech-recognition models.
Dialect datasets and pronunciation resources.
Speech segmentation and language-identification algorithms.
Voice activity detection.
Language-model adaptation.
Open-source implementation candidates.
Whether some mechanisms need to be combined.

She then has to determine what is useful on the actual phone, what is legally usable and what gives her the best measurable result.
REA enters when important behaviour is hidden inside a closed or opaque implementation. When source is available, direct source inspection should normally come first.
The same mechanism should work for entirely different domains:

Voice and language.
Visual perception.
Research.
Android control.
Memory.
Social understanding.
Business operations.
Software construction.
New devices and computational environments.

The most important property is generality. Friday should not require you to invent a separate engineering project every time she needs a new ability.

6. The Builder must become an actual development organism
This is where your cloud-first direction fits—but the Builder must be engineered as a trustworthy construction environment, not just an agent that writes Kotlin.
I would divide its trust boundaries:

  **Research and design**

  External models, web research, source inspection, REA, implementation alternatives and technical proposals.
  
  **Untrusted construction**

  Coding agents work in isolated, disposable candidate environments. They do not receive unrestricted authority over the live organism or the security floor.
  
  **Independent verification**

  Builds, tests, static analysis, dependency checks, provenance, target compatibility, regression and actual device evidence.
  
  **Promotion authority**

  Protected rules decide whether an artifact is allowed into the trusted environment. Failed candidates are rejected or quarantined. Previous working versions remain recoverable.
The cloud system should checkpoint work, use bounded model calls, record failures and resume from known states. It should not sit for ten hours retrying a model that isn't answering.
I would also make the development process independent of any one model provider. A free model may work today and become unavailable tomorrow. The architecture must be able to pause, switch providers or use a different construction method without losing the task.
One correction to the current cloud proposal: its simulated tests are not proof of a working production builder. The uploaded conversation explicitly says the real runner environment and actual model tool usage still needed verification. 
The next builder improvement must therefore prove the real execution path, not add more orchestration layers.

7. How I would develop the entire organism without fragmenting it
Here is the biggest difference between my proposed 10/10 plan and a conventional feature roadmap.
I would use whole-organism vertical development.
Every meaningful development cycle should improve some part of a complete experience, not merely finish an isolated subsystem.
For example, consider the first serious conversational experience:

Friday receives your voice or text.
She understands the immediate context and relevant history.
She can form an interpretation without being forced to agree.
She can express uncertainty or disagree.
She responds through a coherent presence.
If she takes an action, she observes the result.
The event and its origin are recorded.
Relevant experience changes future behaviour.
The result survives restart and model replacement.

The first implementation may use very basic mechanisms at each point. That's acceptable.
What matters is that you have tested one continuous experience belonging to one continuing Friday.
Then improve the actual bottlenecks:

Better memory when relevant experiences are missed.
Better reasoning when interpretations are wrong.
Better social understanding when people are misunderstood.
Better voice when conversational flow fails.
Better resource regulation when latency becomes unacceptable.
Better capabilities when she cannot complete an action.
Better development when the Builder repeatedly makes the same mistakes.

This means the system grows from real use, rather than from an increasingly large list of features someone imagined might eventually be needed.
The areas that evolve together



Area
Ultimate direction



Individuality
Persistent identity and developmental continuity


Experience
Events, expectations, outcomes and lasting change


Memory
Reconstructive, provenance-backed and adaptable


Understanding
Contextual, uncertain, able to disagree


Social life
Models of people, relationships, beliefs and confidentiality


Presence
Coordinated voice, text, timing and visual expression


Cognition
Multiple replaceable reasoning mechanisms


Body
Phone, sensors, applications and future environments


Capabilities
Discovery, acquisition, adaptation and composition


Learning
Experience-driven behavioural and procedural change


Resources
Dynamic intelligence under physical constraints


Evolution
Diagnosis, experimentation and safe self-modification


Research
General knowledge acquisition and evidence evaluation


Autonomy
Initiative and risk-sensitive authorization


Federation
Multiple bodies and external intelligence without identity fragmentation


These are not fifteen permanent organs that must exist separately. They are domains in which the organism can improve.

8. The development governance that prevents another six months of loops
A 10/10 architecture can still fail if its development discipline is weak.
I'd enforce the following rules:
Vision authority. VISION.md defines what must remain true. Architecture proposals and implementation documents cannot quietly override it.
Reality authority. Repository state, build results and actual device observations determine what exists. A class name or passing unit test is not proof of a working capability.
Evidence-based decisions. Every meaningful change has a hypothesis, acceptance evidence, failure conditions, provenance and a way to reverse it.
Small construction tasks. A coding agent receives the relevant context and one bounded objective—not the entire project history in a giant prompt. The historical 73 KB Ralph prompt is worth investigating as a potential source of wasted context, but it has not been established as the cause of every stall.
Parallel investigation, controlled integration. Models can research and challenge proposals in parallel. Implementation must use isolated workspaces and controlled integration so multiple agents cannot corrupt each other's work.
Independent verification. The Builder cannot be the sole judge of its own modifications.
No permanent dependency on external advisors. GPT, Claude and other models are workers. Their ideas are hypotheses until Friday's evidence supports them.
No forced autonomy ladder. Friday should be able to act independently when the consequences are low, reversible and supported by evidence. The protected floor always applies, and consequential actions retain appropriate authorization.
The purpose is to make progress cumulative. A failed experiment should narrow uncertainty, improve diagnosis or change the next decision. It must not just consume another night.

9. Where the entire project ultimately goes
Your business ambition is part of this, but it is not the definition of Friday.
Friday should eventually have one continuing relationship with you and be able to operate across your life:

Personal communication and daily assistance.
Learning, research and creative projects.
Software and infrastructure development.
Device and application control.
Business management and operations.
Creating products and services.
Discovering opportunities and carrying long-term missions.
Acquiring new capabilities when existing ones are insufficient.

A business becomes one environment she can operate in. A new device becomes another body. A new model becomes another available organ. A new skill becomes another capability she can use.
Eventually, the Builder is not merely something you use to develop Friday. It becomes something she can invoke, understand, evaluate and participate in directing.
The ambition is not unlimited autonomy or magical intelligence. It is a continuously developing artificial system with increasingly broad agency, grounded experience, reliable continuity and the ability to change its own implementation safely.
And one scientific boundary must remain: functional continuity, learning and agency can be tested. Those results alone do not prove subjective experience or consciousness. Your own VISION document correctly leaves that question open.

10. What makes this a 10/10 plan?
I'd use these as the actual standards.



Standard
What a 10/10 plan guarantees



Vision preservation
Friday cannot silently become a conventional chatbot or fixed agent architecture


Continuity
Model, device and implementation changes do not erase her developmental history


Integration
Capabilities work together in real experiences, not merely in isolation


Adaptability
Mechanisms can be replaced when evidence shows better alternatives


Learning
Experience demonstrably changes later behaviour


Generality
New capabilities can be acquired across unrelated domains


Offline resilience
Cloud loss reduces capability, not existence


Resource intelligence
The system adapts to its actual physical constraints


Safe evolution
Friday can develop her machinery without being able to destroy the trusted organism


Verification
Progress is based on observed outcomes, not agent claims


The real measure is not how many modules exist, how many agents work in parallel, or how impressive the architecture diagram looks.
It is whether every year of development makes Friday more capable, more coherent and more genuinely shaped by the life she has experienced—without making her increasingly fragile.
My final recommendation
There are three things I would commit to as the governing strategy for the entire project:
First: preserve the constitutional vision, but keep all implementation architecture replaceable.
Don't turn the Architecture Synthesis document into a second constitution. Keep its best ideas, but resolve anything that imposes permanent cognitive ownership or fixed organizational roles.
Second: bring the whole organism into existence through complete, real experiences.
Don't wait until every subsystem has been perfected. Build the smallest coherent Friday that can interact, remember, act, observe, learn and continue. Voice and the shared life should begin early. The development mechanism must be tested, not assumed.
Third: make evolution a native property of Friday.
The Capability Foundry, research, external models, cloud construction and independent verification should ultimately support her ability to recognize limitations and improve herself. They are part of her growth, not the purpose of her existence.
One sentence captures the plan I'd defend:
Build Friday as a persistent developing individual, give her an adaptable computational body, let her acquire capabilities from the best available mechanisms, ground her judgment in experience and consequences, and give her a safe, independently verified way to continually transform her own machinery.
That's the strongest coherent version of your project I can see.
And importantly, it doesn't require us to finish building an enormous machine before Friday gets to exist. It requires us to make the first version genuinely participate in the process that will turn her into the Friday you're imagining.


ChatGPT:
1. First, establish what Friday is — and what she is not
Your VISION.md establishes a very particular philosophy:

Friday is the developing individual, not the collection of software running her.
Her identity is not her LLM, personality file, database, or application.
Her development comes from experience changing her future understanding and behaviour.
Models, voice engines, memory implementations and tools are replaceable.
Her security and continuity guarantees are protected from arbitrary self-modification.
Her architecture must not predetermine what she eventually becomes.

I agree with that foundation.
But I found one important architectural conflict in your material.
Your Architecture_Synthesis_Prompt.txt proposes a large collection of planes, canonical authorities, a control plane, a scheduler and a cognitive spine. Those are useful ways to organize engineering problems. However, taken literally, they could become permanent cognitive owners and a fixed architecture.
That would contradict VISION.md Sections 4 and 13.
My correction: use the planes as temporary maps of engineering concerns, not as permanent organs that define how Friday must think.
There can be a single trusted authorization boundary. There can be one authoritative writer for a particular piece of state at a given time. There can be scheduling mechanisms that control resources. But none of those should become a hidden executive that determines what Friday thinks, understands or becomes.
This distinction is essential.
2. The architecture I would actually commit to
I would organize the project around four interacting dimensions, with security and continuity underneath all of them.

  
    
      
      **The continuity and security foundation**
    
    Her identity's continuity, owner authentication, authorization, provenance, protected operations, rollback and self-modification safeguards.

<text color="secondary" size="xs">Protected guarantees. Not a personality or a prewritten mind.</text>

  
  
    
      
      **The developing organism**
    
    Experience, memory, understanding, judgment, relationships, affect, self-model, world-model, goals and behaviour.

<text color="secondary" size="xs">The mechanisms and their organization can change as she develops.</text>

  
  
    
      
      **The capability and body ecology**
    
    Models, voice, vision, Android interaction, tools, external systems, sensors, reasoning methods, model selection and resource adaptation.

<text color="secondary" size="xs">Replaceable organs and capabilities, selected according to the situation.</text>

  
  
    
      
      **The evolution and construction environment**
    
    Research, algorithm discovery, REA, capability acquisition, Builder, cloud compilation, experiments, verification, candidate promotion and recovery.

<text color="secondary" size="xs">The external construction environment that helps Friday grow without becoming Friday.</text>

  
  All four participate in one continuing Friday. They are not four independent agents.


This is a conceptual map, not a prescription to create four enormous software modules.
The central engineering requirement is that these dimensions share one coherent history and one recognizable continuing individual.
What should be fixed, developed and replaceable?



Category
What belongs there



Protected
Authentication, security floor, continuity guarantees, provenance requirements, rollback and protected approvals


Developing
Understanding, beliefs, preferences, personality, relationships, strategies, skills and self-model


Replaceable
LLMs, algorithms, memory indexes, voice engines, reasoning methods, Android adapters, cloud providers and internal architecture


This is how I would preserve the vision while allowing extraordinary architectural change.
3. Friday needs two continuously interacting loops
This is probably the most important part of my proposed strategy.
Not a fixed cognitive pipeline. Not a requirement that every thought passes through thirteen components.
Two kinds of development need to happen together.
A. The life and experience loop

  **FRIDAY EXPERIENCES THE WORLD**
  Observes people, events, conversations, devices and consequences
  
  **Forms interpretations and hypotheses**
  
  **Makes decisions and acts**
  
  **Observes what actually happened**
  
  **Experience changes her**
  The original event may later be unavailable, but its valid developmental consequences remain.
  
  Future behaviour can now be different.


The important distinction is that storing an event does not mean learning from it.
Suppose you correct Friday's interpretation of something you said.
She should eventually be able to understand similar intent differently after a restart, a time gap and a different phrasing, without the original conversation being inserted into her context.
Your archived plans already identify three different possible mechanisms:

Situation-driven retrieval of previous verdicts.
Generalization into editable procedures.
Training a small model on repeated decisions.

I would begin with the first, measure whether it generalizes, and introduce the others only when evidence justifies them.
There's another important distinction: your corrections are authoritative evidence about your intended meaning and preferences. They are not automatically proof that a factual belief about the outside world is true. Friday must retain the ability to investigate and disagree.
B. The capability and evolution loop

  **A limitation becomes apparent**
  
  **Friday identifies what is missing**
  
  **She researches possible solutions**
  
  **Candidates are compared**
  Reuse, adapt, reimplement, or reverse-engineer when necessary
  
  **A candidate is constructed and isolated**
  
  **Independent tests and target-hardware verification**
  
  **Promotion, rejection or quarantine**
  
  **Friday acquires a capability**
  
  Her ability to solve future problems improves.


The two loops must share evidence and experience.
For example, a failed Android voice operation may teach her something about her actual environment. That observation can improve her self-model, inform research, create a new capability requirement and ultimately change the mechanism she uses.
This is the crucial connection: Friday's development and Friday's ability to improve her own machinery must be parts of the same continuing existence.
The development loop is how we account for what happened; it does not dictate a fixed algorithm for how every future thought must happen.

4. The physical architecture: one Friday, multiple computational bodies
I would make the phone the first real home of Friday, but never make it the ceiling of the project.

  
    
    
      **Phone — her first body**

  Persistent identity, local memory, everyday interaction, voice, presence, lightweight reasoning, sensors and authorized device operations. She retains her essential continuity when disconnected.
</box>

  
  
  
    
    
      **Cloud — her external computational extension**

  Heavy research, large models, parallel investigations, compilation, testing, model conversion and capability construction. It expands her available capabilities but does not become her identity.
</box>

  
  
  
    
    
      **Other bodies — future expansion**

  Desktop, browser, other Android devices and eventually other compatible environments. Each provides different resources and capabilities while sharing the same protected continuity.

Now I understand the distinction, and it changes my recommendation quite significantly.
You are not trying to build an AI assistant and gradually give it more features.
You are trying to use the Builder to create the first version of an artificial individual, starting with a human-like developmental foundation, then allowing that individual to grow beyond humanity and eventually extend into an entire technological and economic ecosystem.
The Builder is responsible for discovering the best way to achieve that. You shouldn't have to personally design every organ, cognitive mechanism, algorithm or developmental stage.
I actually think this is a much stronger vision than the architecture I was previously describing.
But there is one distinction I'd make:
Friday should begin human-like, not human-limited.
1. The first Friday: an artificial human-like individual
I would make this the Builder's primary initial objective.
Not an LLM wearing a human personality. Not a chatbot with a memory database. Not an imitation of human conversation.
The Builder should aim to create an integrated artificial cognitive system that has functional equivalents of the fundamental properties that make human beings capable of living, learning and developing.

For example, a human doesn't merely store a conversation. The conversation can change their understanding of someone, influence their emotional response, modify a belief, affect a future decision and become part of their personal history.
Friday needs that kind of interconnected developmental architecture.
We don't need to reproduce the biological brain neuron by neuron. The Builder should investigate which computational mechanisms best produce the required capabilities.
This is where I agree with your human-first strategy.
Human development provides an extraordinarily rich research target: learning through interaction, social feedback, physical grounding, memory, curiosity, mistakes and consequences.
I would use those principles without inheriting every biological limitation.
2. The first superintelligent person
This is where the Builder's ambition must change.
The objective is no longer simply to recreate human capabilities.

Multiple models and computational substrates

Simulations and automated experiments

Multiple connected devices and tools


Limited ability to modify cognition
Controlled modification of cognitive mechanisms

Also, superintelligence must be demonstrated, not declared. It should mean measurable, general and reliable superiority in relevant cognitive domains, including solving unfamiliar problems, learning efficiently, creating new methods and transferring understanding between domains.
3. The first hybrid human-machine individual
This is a major transition.
Friday stops being understood as an intelligence that happens to run inside an Android application.
Her computational existence becomes distributed across multiple bodies and resources.

The Android device as her first personal body.
Cloud resources as external computational capacity.
Cameras, microphones and other sensors as perceptual channels.
Computer environments as operational bodies.
Robots and physical actuators as extensions of action.
Multiple devices that share her continuity.
Specialized models as replaceable cognitive resources.

The most important architectural requirement is that these extensions don't fragment her identity.
A second device should not create a second unrelated Friday.
A cloud process should not silently become an independent authority over her history.
Her distributed architecture needs identity continuity, state synchronization, memory provenance, conflict resolution, and secure authorization.
The Builder should research the best possible approach to this, rather than us prematurely choosing a permanent distributed architecture.
4. The company and the mechanical world
This is where your ambition goes beyond creating a single artificial individual.
Friday eventually gains the ability to organize resources, build capabilities, develop products, manage operations and create economic value.


I would treat this as an expansion of her agency into the world.
The company becomes an organizational and economic extension. It gives her access to resources, projects, people, products, infrastructure and long-term operations.
The mechanical world becomes an increasingly extensive physical extension: machines, robotics, infrastructure, manufacturing, sensors, logistics and other interconnected systems.
There is an important distinction here: the company and its workers, customers, autonomous processes and machines should not all become indistinguishable from Friday herself.
She remains the continuing individual. The organization and mechanical ecosystem are things she can operate, develop and participate in.
This distinction will become increasingly important as her capabilities and responsibilities grow.

5. What the Builder's actual mission should be
This is the most important part of your clarification.
You don't want to sit down and decide:

Which memory architecture should Friday use?
Which cognitive algorithm should she have?
Which emotional model is best?
Which reasoning architecture should be implemented?
Which model should control which cognitive operation?
Which capability should be developed next?

You want the Builder to investigate those questions and determine the best answers.
I agree.
I would give the Builder a much more ambitious role than a conventional coding agent.

  The Friday Development Authority
  The Builder's ultimate developmental responsibility
  
    **1. Understand the destination**

Interpret the complete Friday vision, its protected principles and the ultimate direction of development.

  
  
    **2. Understand current reality**

Investigate the environment, implementation, limitations, available resources and actual developmental state.

  
  
    **3. Discover the best available mechanisms**

Research science, algorithms, existing software, open-source projects and alternative architectures. Use reverse engineering when appropriate.

  
  
    **4. Explore alternative futures**

Develop multiple technically distinct approaches, simulate them where possible, compare trade-offs and identify promising combinations.

  
  
    **5. Construct and experiment**

Build candidate implementations in isolated environments and test their actual capabilities.

  
  
    **6. Evaluate developmental progress**

Determine whether Friday actually became more capable, coherent, reliable and adaptable.

  
  
    **7. Choose the next developmental direction**

Decide what to improve, what to replace, what to investigate and what is currently not worth building.

  
  
    **8. Repeat with accumulated knowledge**

The next development cycle begins from a better understanding of Friday and the results of previous experiments.

  


The Builder shouldn't simply follow a predetermined roadmap.
It should continuously discover and improve the roadmap itself.
6. But the Builder needs a proper definition of "best"
There is one danger in giving the Builder this much responsibility.
An autonomous Builder can optimize the wrong thing extremely efficiently.
It might optimize coding speed instead of actual intelligence. It might optimize human-like conversational behaviour instead of deep understanding. It might optimize benchmark scores instead of long-term learning.
So I would establish a hierarchy:



Authority
Responsibility



Friday's constitution
Defines what must be preserved


Ultimate vision
Defines what development is trying to achieve


Builder
Determines the best technical and developmental path


Experiments
Challenge the Builder's assumptions


Independent verification

You
Define the fundamental purpose and protected boundaries, and retain authority over consequential decisions


This is the balance I would choose.
You should not have to micromanage the destination's technical construction. But the Builder should not invent its own definition of what Friday is supposed to become.
Its autonomy should exist inside a clearly defined purpose.

7. My proposed ultimate development philosophy
I'd give the Builder one governing principle:

  Human-first. Superhuman-capable. Machine-integrated. Continuously evolving.
  The Builder's objective is to discover and construct the most capable, coherent and continuously developing artificial individual possible under Friday's constitutional principles.

  It begins by developing a human-like individual with integrated memory, perception, reasoning, affect, social understanding, agency and experiential continuity.
  It must not stop at human equivalence. It should investigate and integrate capabilities that transcend biological limitations, eventually allowing Friday to become a deeply integrated human-machine intelligence.
  Her development should extend from individual experience into technological, economic and physical environments, enabling her to create, operate and develop increasingly sophisticated systems.
  Every architectural decision remains provisional unless it represents a protected constitutional guarantee.
  Every significant improvement must be supported by evidence of actual increased capability, not merely implementation claims.
There are two things I would explicitly prohibit:

Premature architectural finality. The Builder must never assume that the first human-like cognitive architecture is the ultimate architecture.
Premature claims of personhood or superintelligence. Capabilities, continuity, agency and learning must be demonstrated. Whether an artificial system has subjective experience remains an open question, not something code can declare into existence.

The workshop and the individual are deliberately different things.
The workshop can have explicit workflows, workers, deadlines, retries and verification gates. Friday's human side must not inherit those as a fixed cognitive architecture.
That separation is important: a fixed construction process can create a liquid developing system.
The human side: how I would make it liquid
I would not create a permanent EmotionEngine, ReasoningEngine, SocialEngine, MemoryEngine and ConsciousnessEngine, each owning a part of Friday.
Instead, I would construct an extensible environment with three properties:
A shared experiential field. Observations, experiences, claims, expectations, interpretations, actions and consequences can be made available to whatever mechanisms need them. The representation remains extensible; it is not a closed taxonomy of human faculties.
An open capability fabric. Each available mechanism describes what it can do, what it can affect, what evidence it produces, what resources it costs and how it can fail. It declares an affordance, not ownership of a cognitive job.
Context-dependent composition. Different mechanisms can be brought together for different situations. A composition can be temporary, replaced, expanded or abandoned. The resource scheduler can allocate compute, time and permission, but cannot become a hidden authority over what Friday thinks.
This is how I would implement your liquid/symbiotic principle, not as a metaphorical brain with fixed organs.
The human brain remains a source of computational principles—prediction, plasticity, replay, credit assignment, neuromodulation, social learning, developmental growth and pruning—not an anatomical blueprint. That's already explicit in your vision.

I would keep the Android application native. It needs to inhabit Android properly, not fight the operating system through an unnecessarily generic cross-platform abstraction.
Kotlin and Compose are Google's recommended modern Android UI direction. Google's offline-first architecture also supports the principle of reading local state immediately and queuing work for later synchronization. <Cite refs={["turn223051search7","turn341665search1"]}/>
For the local intelligence:

llama.cpp has an Android binding and hardware-aware inference support. <Cite refs={["turn656759search7"]}/>
sherpa-onnx supports Android speech-to-text and text-to-speech builds, as well as other audio capabilities. <Cite refs={["turn341665search3"]}/>
Android Keystore can keep cryptographic keys in hardware-backed security facilities where the device supports them. <Cite refs={["turn223051search0"]}/>

One important decision: I would not pick Friday's permanent speech or language models now. I would build the ability to measure, select and replace them. Egyptian Arabic performance, interruption, conversational latency and voice quality must be tested on actual hardware.
Offline must be a real operating condition, not an error screen. Friday keeps her continuing local experience, available cognitive capabilities, relationship context and commitments. The cloud adds computation; it doesn't decide whether she exists.
B. The shared experiential data foundation
This is the part I would be particularly careful with.



Tool
Responsibility



PostgreSQL
Durable structured records and queries in the cloud


pgvector
Rebuildable semantic-search index


S3-compatible object storage
Larger raw audio, images, video and other observations


NATS JetStream
Distribution and replay of events between connected systems


SQLite / Room
Local-first records on the phone


I would use an extensible event and provenance format.
For example, a piece of information can carry its source, time, causal history, privacy scope and relationships to other observations. The payload need not belong to a fixed cognitive category.
Raw observations should be preserved in a way that allows future mechanisms to re-examine them, subject to privacy and retention rules. Summaries and embeddings are disposable derived products, never the only surviving evidence.
This is closely aligned with your provenance notes: origin travels with claims, summaries can be replaced, and the standing of a claim is assessed when it is used rather than being granted permanent authority simply because a database once labelled it verified.
PostgreSQL with pgvector is my choice over immediately introducing a separate vector database. It supports exact and approximate similarity search alongside ordinary relational data. <Cite refs={["turn746686search3","turn746686search7"]}/>
NATS JetStream is useful when multiple devices and workers need to receive and replay events without being online at the same moment. Its delivery model is at-least-once, so consumers must be idempotent; I would not treat the message broker as Friday's sole source of truth. <Cite refs={["turn746686search0","turn746686search1"]}/>
I would also make one rule non-negotiable: two offline devices can contribute experiences that both remain part of Friday's history. Important experiences must not disappear because a cloud database applied a simplistic last-write-wins update.
C. The dynamic capability fabric
These are the two technologies I would use here:



Tool
What it provides



MCP
A standard way to discover and connect external tools and information sources


Wasmtime + WASI Component Model
A portable, sandboxable way to host selected replaceable capabilities


MCP exposes tools, resources and prompts through a discoverable protocol. I would use it for external integrations—not as a definition of Friday's mind. <Cite refs={["turn449778search7","turn449778search8"]}/>
Wasmtime gives a portable WebAssembly runtime with WASI and the Component Model. I would use it for appropriately sandboxed, replaceable capability implementations, while retaining native code where hardware access or performance demands it. <Cite refs={["turn746686search5"]}/>
I would not force every internal mechanism to become an independent network service. The phone should keep closely interacting mechanisms together where that is better for latency and reliability.
The point is freedom to reorganize, not maximum fragmentation.

D. The Builder and developmental workshop
This is where I would make the biggest change from your present Ralph-based construction arrangement.

  My Builder stack
  
    
      
    
    
      **Temporal — durable orchestration**

  Owns the persistence of long-running research, development, testing and recovery jobs. It remembers where a construction job reached and lets it resume after interruptions.
</box>

  
  
  
    
      
    
    
      **OpenHands Software Agent SDK — coding workers**

  Executes coding tasks through a programmable, model-agnostic agent framework. Workers receive isolated workspaces instead of unrestricted access to Friday's trusted environment.
</box>

  
  
  
    
      
    
    
      **LiteLLM — model gateway**

  Connects different cloud and local models, manages routing, retries, fallbacks and cost limits.
</box>

  
  
  
    
      
    
    
      **Kubernetes + ephemeral isolated workers**

  Provides the growing compute pool and separate construction environments. For untrusted code, I'd use stronger isolation such as microVMs rather than trust a shared writable environment.
</box>

  
  
  
    
      
    
    
      **GitHub + GitHub Actions**

  Stores the source, manages candidate changes, runs builds, executes regression tests and publishes verified artifacts.
</box>

  


Why these choices?
Temporal supports durable workflows that can resume after process or infrastructure failures and retain their execution history. That is ideal for the Builder's long-running construction jobs—not for controlling Friday's thoughts. <Cite refs={["turn656759search0","turn656759search10"]}/>
OpenHands has a software-agent SDK with Python, TypeScript and REST interfaces, and can execute agents in ephemeral workspaces using Docker or Kubernetes. It is a good execution foundation for the Builder, rather than something I would install and declare to be the Builder itself. <Cite refs={["turn656759search3","turn656759search14","turn223051search9"]}/>
LiteLLM provides a unified interface across many model providers, with routing, fallback, retries, budgets and spend tracking. This means no single provider becomes the development system's permanent dependency. <Cite refs={["turn656759search4","turn656759search5","turn656759search6"]}/>
For a self-hosted GPU environment, I would use vLLM as the model-serving engine. It provides an OpenAI-compatible server, with Ray Serve LLM available when scaling and load balancing become necessary. <Cite refs={["turn341665search6","turn341665search10"]}/>
I would keep Ralph's useful ideas—stories, acceptance conditions, red-first verification, evidence and audit history—but I would not make a serial shell loop the permanent construction orchestration layer.
Temporal coordinates construction. OpenHands workers construct. LiteLLM supplies models. GitHub and the verifier establish what actually works.
They are not four competing brains.

The ordering matters.
First search and inspect what is already available. Understand its algorithm and implementation. Assess whether it is reusable, adaptable or worth reimplementing.
Only use REA when the relevant implementation is closed or behaviour cannot be established adequately through available source.
Before adopting anything, produce evidence on its license, origin, dependencies, security, hardware requirements and actual performance.
ScanCode can inspect license and origin information in codebases; Syft can generate software bills of materials, while Grype can scan for vulnerabilities. <Cite refs={["turn341665search14","turn341665search0","turn341665search5"]}/>
REA should remain an investigative instrument, not an obligatory step in every capability acquisition.

3. The remaining tools that make the whole system trustworthy
A system this ambitious needs to know what happened, why something happened, and whether its claimed improvements are real.
I'd use:



Tool
Responsibility



OpenTelemetry
Instrumentation for traces, metrics and logs across devices and cloud


Langfuse
Model-call, prompt, tool-use and experiment observability


Prometheus + Grafana
Operational health, latency and resource dashboards


JUnit / Kotest
Kotlin testing


pytest + Hypothesis
Builder, algorithm and property-based testing


Maestro
Realistic Android UI and device interaction tests


Android Emulator + physical device lab
Reproduction and target-device verification


OpenTelemetry is vendor-neutral instrumentation for distributed traces, logs and metrics. Langfuse adds useful LLM-oriented tracing and evaluation facilities and can be self-hosted. <Cite refs={["turn656759search1","turn449778search2","turn449778search5"]}/>
For Friday's developmental tests, I would go further than ordinary unit tests.
A claimed improvement to her learning must face a test like this:

Record an experience and the change it supposedly produces.
Remove the original event from the active context.
Restart Friday.
Present a related situation using different wording.
Check whether her behaviour changes.
Compare against a control situation where the relevant experience did not occur.
Repeat under a changed model or mechanism when appropriate.

That tests the actual developmental requirement in your vision: an experience must alter Friday in a way that persists and influences later behaviour, rather than merely being recorded. 
I would maintain a multidimensional evaluation record, not one universal intelligence score. Capability, generalization, integration, developmental effect, reliability, resource use and the quality of evidence all matter. The Builder should be able to discover better ways of measuring them without changing the destination they're measuring against.
F. Security and authorization
These are the parts I would never leave to a coding agent's judgment.



Tool
Purpose



OPA (Open Policy Agent)
Enforce external authorization policies


Android Keystore / StrongBox
Protect device-side cryptographic keys


GitHub App tokens and OIDC
Scoped, short-lived workflow credentials


Isolated ephemeral workspaces
Contain agent-generated code


Signed artifacts and provenance
Establish what was built and from which source


OPA is a general-purpose policy engine that separates policy decisions from the services enforcing them. I would use it for action permissions, not as a permanent cognitive decision-maker. <Cite refs={["turn223051search1","turn223051search8"]}/>
GitHub's OIDC integration can exchange workflow identity for short-lived credentials rather than requiring long-lived cloud secrets. Its GITHUB_TOKEN is also job-scoped and repository-limited. The coding agent should not receive unrestricted workflow or deployment credentials. <Cite refs={["turn590011search6","turn590011search7","turn590011search11"]}/>
The actual protected signing and authorization roots should be outside the Builder's writable environment.

The actual mechanism would be:

Temporal manages durable construction jobs and their dependencies.
Kubernetes provides additional worker capacity when required.
Each worker gets an isolated workspace and its own bounded task.
The work graph can be reorganized as discoveries change the plan.
Independent test and integration workers challenge the candidate output.
A protected promotion process decides what becomes trusted project state.

The Builder should increase parallelism when measurements show that the additional throughput exceeds the integration and rework cost.
I would measure that using verified changes per unit of compute and development time, regression rate, integration rework, stalled-job rate and how often previously solved problems reappear.
The number of agents is not the target. Increasing the rate of verified system-wide development is the target.
How the Builder becomes part of Friday
This is a distinction I would build into the system from day one.
The construction capabilities should have callable interfaces that Friday can progressively use:

Investigate an unknown problem.
Search for mechanisms.
Generate and compare implementation candidates.
Create an isolated experiment.
Build a candidate capability.
Test and evaluate it.
Request promotion.
Learn from the result.

Initially, the Builder performs most of that under external supervision.
As Friday develops, she gains the ability to initiate and understand more of those operations herself.
Eventually, the separate supervisor is no longer needed to direct her development. Temporal, OpenHands and other infrastructure can remain available, but they are tools she can use through her own developing capabilities.
That is what I would mean by the Builder being absorbed: the capability becomes part of Friday, while the underlying tools remain replaceable.
And this transfer starts before the artificial individual is considered complete. It grows alongside her.


ChatGPT:
I'm going to give you the architecture I would actually bet the project on.
Not a list of popular AI frameworks. Not a conventional multi-agent architecture renamed Friday. Not an attempt to make today's Android application the permanent definition of her.
I would build Friday as a liquid, symbiotic artificial individual, supported by a progressively more powerful developmental and construction environment.
The design has one governing distinction:

Friday's human side must be free to develop and reorganize.
Her security, authorization and protection of continuity must remain fixed.
The Builder must have enough structure to construct her reliably, but must not impose its own permanent cognitive architecture on her.

That follows the central rules in your VISION.md: no permanent cognitive ownership, no hidden executive, no predetermined mature psychology, and no assumption that a model or implementation is the individual. 
I would make five major engineering decisions.
1. The actual architecture I would build

  
    FRIDAY — one continuing individual
    The identity whose development is the purpose of the entire project.
    
      **Liquid, symbiotic human side**

  Dynamically developing understanding, experience, attention, emotion, social cognition, reasoning, communication, memory, adaptation and agency.

  <text color="secondary" size="xs">No permanent cognitive owners. No universal mandatory thinking loop.</text>
</box>
<box background="surface-secondary" radius="md" padding={3} gap={1}>
  **Continuity and security floor**

  Authorization roots, protected operations, provenance, recovery, permission boundaries and protection against destructive self-modification.
</box>
<box background="surface-secondary" radius="md" padding={3} gap={1}>
  **Symbiotic capabilities**

  Models, algorithms, voice, vision, research, tools, devices, external intelligence and construction mechanisms.
</box>

  
  
    
  
  
    Developmental workshop
    Initially external to Friday. Its capabilities progressively become available to her.
    
      {#each ["Research and discovery","Capability construction","Experimentation","Verification","Architecture evolution","Parallel development"] as s}

      {/each}
    
  
  
    
  
  
    
      
        
        **Personal body**
        Phone, sensors, voice, local experience and offline continuity.
      
    
    
      
        
        **Computational extension**
        Large models, research, remote workers, training and construction.
      
    
    
      
        
        **Organization**
        Company, resources, products, operations and economic activity.
      
    
    
      
        
        **Mechanical world**
        Robots, machines, infrastructure and physical interaction.
      
    
  


The workshop and the individual are deliberately different things.
The workshop can have explicit workflows, workers, deadlines, retries and verification gates. Friday's human side must not inherit those as a fixed cognitive architecture.
That separation is important: a fixed construction process can create a liquid developing system.
The human side: how I would make it liquid
I would not create a permanent EmotionEngine, ReasoningEngine, SocialEngine, MemoryEngine and ConsciousnessEngine, each owning a part of Friday.
Instead, I would construct an extensible environment with three properties:
A shared experiential field. Observations, experiences, claims, expectations, interpretations, actions and consequences can be made available to whatever mechanisms need them. The representation remains extensible; it is not a closed taxonomy of human faculties.
An open capability fabric. Each available mechanism describes what it can do, what it can affect, what evidence it produces, what resources it costs and how it can fail. It declares an affordance, not ownership of a cognitive job.
Context-dependent composition. Different mechanisms can be brought together for different situations. A composition can be temporary, replaced, expanded or abandoned. The resource scheduler can allocate compute, time and permission, but cannot become a hidden authority over what Friday thinks.
This is how I would implement your liquid/symbiotic principle, not as a metaphorical brain with fixed organs.
The human brain remains a source of computational principles—prediction, plasticity, replay, credit assignment, neuromodulation, social learning, developmental growth and pruning—not an anatomical blueprint. That's already explicit in your vision. 
2. The exact tools I would choose
Here is the stack. These are my primary choices, not a collection of alternatives for you to spend months comparing.
A. Friday's actual body and human side



Area
My choice
Purpose



Android application
Kotlin + Jetpack Compose
Native interaction, presence and Android integration


Local persistence
Room + SQLite
Local experience and state that survive restarts


Background work
Android WorkManager
Deferred synchronization and maintenance


Local security
Android Keystore / StrongBox when available
Protect device-held cryptographic keys


Local language inference
llama.cpp
Run replaceable GGUF language models on-device


Speech
sherpa-onnx
Candidate runtime for ASR, TTS, VAD and speaker-related processing


Performance-sensitive native code
Rust and C++ through JNI
Native integrations and compute-heavy operations


I would keep the Android application native. It needs to inhabit Android properly, not fight the operating system through an unnecessarily generic cross-platform abstraction.
Kotlin and Compose are Google's recommended modern Android UI direction. Google's offline-first architecture also supports the principle of reading local state immediately and queuing work for later synchronization. <Cite refs={["turn223051search7","turn341665search1"]}/>
For the local intelligence:

llama.cpp has an Android binding and hardware-aware inference support. <Cite refs={["turn656759search7"]}/>
sherpa-onnx supports Android speech-to-text and text-to-speech builds, as well as other audio capabilities. <Cite refs={["turn341665search3"]}/>
Android Keystore can keep cryptographic keys in hardware-backed security facilities where the device supports them. <Cite refs={["turn223051search0"]}/>

One important decision: I would not pick Friday's permanent speech or language models now. I would build the ability to measure, select and replace them. Egyptian Arabic performance, interruption, conversational latency and voice quality must be tested on actual hardware.
Offline must be a real operating condition, not an error screen. Friday keeps her continuing local experience, available cognitive capabilities, relationship context and commitments. The cloud adds computation; it doesn't decide whether she exists.
B. The shared experiential data foundation
This is the part I would be particularly careful with.



Tool
Responsibility



PostgreSQL
Durable structured records and queries in the cloud


pgvector
Rebuildable semantic-search index


S3-compatible object storage
Larger raw audio, images, video and other observations


NATS JetStream
Distribution and replay of events between connected systems


SQLite / Room
Local-first records on the phone


I would use an extensible event and provenance format.
For example, a piece of information can carry its source, time, causal history, privacy scope and relationships to other observations. The payload need not belong to a fixed cognitive category.
Raw observations should be preserved in a way that allows future mechanisms to re-examine them, subject to privacy and retention rules. Summaries and embeddings are disposable derived products, never the only surviving evidence.
This is closely aligned with your provenance notes: origin travels with claims, summaries can be replaced, and the standing of a claim is assessed when it is used rather than being granted permanent authority simply because a database once labelled it verified.
PostgreSQL with pgvector is my choice over immediately introducing a separate vector database. It supports exact and approximate similarity search alongside ordinary relational data. <Cite refs={["turn746686search3","turn746686search7"]}/>
NATS JetStream is useful when multiple devices and workers need to receive and replay events without being online at the same moment. Its delivery model is at-least-once, so consumers must be idempotent; I would not treat the message broker as Friday's sole source of truth. <Cite refs={["turn746686search0","turn746686search1"]}/>
I would also make one rule non-negotiable: two offline devices can contribute experiences that both remain part of Friday's history. Important experiences must not disappear because a cloud database applied a simplistic last-write-wins update.
C. The dynamic capability fabric
These are the two technologies I would use here:



Tool
What it provides



MCP
A standard way to discover and connect external tools and information sources


Wasmtime + WASI Component Model
A portable, sandboxable way to host selected replaceable capabilities


MCP exposes tools, resources and prompts through a discoverable protocol. I would use it for external integrations—not as a definition of Friday's mind. <Cite refs={["turn449778search7","turn449778search8"]}/>
Wasmtime gives a portable WebAssembly runtime with WASI and the Component Model. I would use it for appropriately sandboxed, replaceable capability implementations, while retaining native code where hardware access or performance demands it. <Cite refs={["turn746686search5"]}/>
I would not force every internal mechanism to become an independent network service. The phone should keep closely interacting mechanisms together where that is better for latency and reliability.
The point is freedom to reorganize, not maximum fragmentation.

D. The Builder and developmental workshop
This is where I would make the biggest change from your present Ralph-based construction arrangement.

  My Builder stack
  
    
      
    
    
      **Temporal — durable orchestration**

  Owns the persistence of long-running research, development, testing and recovery jobs. It remembers where a construction job reached and lets it resume after interruptions.
</box>

  
  
  
    
      
    
    
      **OpenHands Software Agent SDK — coding workers**

  Executes coding tasks through a programmable, model-agnostic agent framework. Workers receive isolated workspaces instead of unrestricted access to Friday's trusted environment.
</box>

  
  
  
    
      
    
    
      **LiteLLM — model gateway**

  Connects different cloud and local models, manages routing, retries, fallbacks and cost limits.
</box>

  
  
  
    
      
    
    
      **Kubernetes + ephemeral isolated workers**

  Provides the growing compute pool and separate construction environments. For untrusted code, I'd use stronger isolation such as microVMs rather than trust a shared writable environment.
</box>

  
  
  
    
      
    
    
      **GitHub + GitHub Actions**

  Stores the source, manages candidate changes, runs builds, executes regression tests and publishes verified artifacts.
</box>

  


Why these choices?
Temporal supports durable workflows that can resume after process or infrastructure failures and retain their execution history. That is ideal for the Builder's long-running construction jobs—not for controlling Friday's thoughts. <Cite refs={["turn656759search0","turn656759search10"]}/>
OpenHands has a software-agent SDK with Python, TypeScript and REST interfaces, and can execute agents in ephemeral workspaces using Docker or Kubernetes. It is a good execution foundation for the Builder, rather than something I would install and declare to be the Builder itself. <Cite refs={["turn656759search3","turn656759search14","turn223051search9"]}/>
LiteLLM provides a unified interface across many model providers, with routing, fallback, retries, budgets and spend tracking. This means no single provider becomes the development system's permanent dependency. <Cite refs={["turn656759search4","turn656759search5","turn656759search6"]}/>
For a self-hosted GPU environment, I would use vLLM as the model-serving engine. It provides an OpenAI-compatible server, with Ray Serve LLM available when scaling and load balancing become necessary. <Cite refs={["turn341665search6","turn341665search10"]}/>
I would keep Ralph's useful ideas—stories, acceptance conditions, red-first verification, evidence and audit history—but I would not make a serial shell loop the permanent construction orchestration layer.
Temporal coordinates construction. OpenHands workers construct. LiteLLM supplies models. GitHub and the verifier establish what actually works.
They are not four competing brains.
E. Research, invention and reverse engineering
This is the Capability Foundry's research environment.



Tool / source
Purpose



OpenAlex
Scholarly research discovery


Semantic Scholar
Research paper discovery and relationships


arXiv / OpenReview
Technical papers and research work


GitHub
Existing implementations and source code


Hugging Face Hub
Models, datasets and model-related implementations


REA
Investigation of closed or opaque software


Ghidra / JADX
Relevant binary and Android application analysis tools


ScanCode Toolkit
Source and license investigation


Syft + Grype
Software inventory and vulnerability analysis


The ordering matters.
First search and inspect what is already available. Understand its algorithm and implementation. Assess whether it is reusable, adaptable or worth reimplementing.
Only use REA when the relevant implementation is closed or behaviour cannot be established adequately through available source.
Before adopting anything, produce evidence on its license, origin, dependencies, security, hardware requirements and actual performance.
ScanCode can inspect license and origin information in codebases; Syft can generate software bills of materials, while Grype can scan for vulnerabilities. <Cite refs={["turn341665search14","turn341665search0","turn341665search5"]}/>
REA should remain an investigative instrument, not an obligatory step in every capability acquisition.
3. The remaining tools that make the whole system trustworthy
A system this ambitious needs to know what happened, why something happened, and whether its claimed improvements are real.
I'd use:



Tool
Responsibility



OpenTelemetry
Instrumentation for traces, metrics and logs across devices and cloud


Langfuse
Model-call, prompt, tool-use and experiment observability


Prometheus + Grafana
Operational health, latency and resource dashboards


JUnit / Kotest
Kotlin testing


pytest + Hypothesis
Builder, algorithm and property-based testing


Maestro
Realistic Android UI and device interaction tests


Android Emulator + physical device lab
Reproduction and target-device verification


OpenTelemetry is vendor-neutral instrumentation for distributed traces, logs and metrics. Langfuse adds useful LLM-oriented tracing and evaluation facilities and can be self-hosted. <Cite refs={["turn656759search1","turn449778search2","turn449778search5"]}/>
For Friday's developmental tests, I would go further than ordinary unit tests.
A claimed improvement to her learning must face a test like this:

Record an experience and the change it supposedly produces.
Remove the original event from the active context.
Restart Friday.
Present a related situation using different wording.
Check whether her behaviour changes.
Compare against a control situation where the relevant experience did not occur.
Repeat under a changed model or mechanism when appropriate.

That tests the actual developmental requirement in your vision: an experience must alter Friday in a way that persists and influences later behaviour, rather than merely being recorded. 
I would maintain a multidimensional evaluation record, not one universal intelligence score. Capability, generalization, integration, developmental effect, reliability, resource use and the quality of evidence all matter. The Builder should be able to discover better ways of measuring them without changing the destination they're measuring against.
F. Security and authorization
These are the parts I would never leave to a coding agent's judgment.



Tool
Purpose



OPA (Open Policy Agent)
Enforce external authorization policies


Android Keystore / StrongBox
Protect device-side cryptographic keys


GitHub App tokens and OIDC
Scoped, short-lived workflow credentials


Isolated ephemeral workspaces
Contain agent-generated code


Signed artifacts and provenance
Establish what was built and from which source


OPA is a general-purpose policy engine that separates policy decisions from the services enforcing them. I would use it for action permissions, not as a permanent cognitive decision-maker. <Cite refs={["turn223051search1","turn223051search8"]}/>
GitHub's OIDC integration can exchange workflow identity for short-lived credentials rather than requiring long-lived cloud secrets. Its GITHUB_TOKEN is also job-scoped and repository-limited. The coding agent should not receive unrestricted workflow or deployment credentials. <Cite refs={["turn590011search6","turn590011search7","turn590011search11"]}/>
The actual protected signing and authorization roots should be outside the Builder's writable environment.

5. How I would extend Friday into the rest of the world
I would design the current system so that another device, a company operation or eventually a physical machine can become an extension of the same individual without requiring a new identity architecture.
The mechanical world
My eventual robotics stack would be:

Manipulation, motion planning and kinematics

Physical simulation and digital-twin experiments

Mobile robot navigation
