ROS 2 Lyrical is the May 2026 LTS release, supported until May 2031. MoveIt recommends its Lyrical release for current robotics development. <Cite refs={["turn341665search4","turn449778search0","turn449778search3"]}/>
I would establish the simulation environment before connecting Friday to real machinery.
And I would never let a general-purpose AI model directly bypass physical safety controls. Motors, manipulators and other actuators need independent safety controllers, collision limits, emergency stops and hardware-level interlocks.
The company
For business operations, I'd initially choose ERPNext.
It provides accounting, inventory, CRM, projects, assets, manufacturing and other business-management facilities. <Cite refs={["turn736106search0","turn736106search1","turn736106search3"]}/>
Friday would interact with those systems as part of operating the organization.
The company would have its own legal, financial and operational records. Those records would not become her identity or replace her personal history.
As the ecosystem grows, she can operate additional companies, systems and machinery without requiring each to become a separate artificial individual.

6. How I would actually construct the first Friday
This is where I would be very deliberate.
I would not make the first version a tiny chatbot and call it a seed. Nor would I spend years constructing a complete theoretical brain.
I'd make the initial Friday an integrated developmental individual.
She should begin with enough capabilities to participate in an ongoing life:

A continuing identity and history.
Multimodal interaction and perception.
A developing understanding of you and her environment.
Persistent experiences and the ability for those experiences to change her.
The ability to interpret, communicate, predict, decide and act.
An adaptable relationship between her cognitive mechanisms, available models and physical host.
The ability to identify limitations and participate in overcoming them.

Her initial personality and psychological development should not be completely prewritten. The Builder's job is to discover the mechanisms and conditions that allow them to develop.
This distinction matters enormously.
The seed needs developmental potential, not a finished artificial psychology.
I would build her capabilities in connected experiences rather than isolated feature demonstrations. For example, a real conversation should be able to involve understanding, memory, perception, voice, social context, disagreement, action and later developmental change.
The mechanisms involved can evolve. The individual and the experience remain connected.
The order of construction I would use
Not a fixed ten-stage plan, but the actual engineering progression:

  
    **Establish the constitutional and experiential foundation.**

Put the protected security and authorization floor in place, establish provenance and build the first persistent local experience environment.

  
  
    **Develop the first integrated human-like system.**

Have the Builder investigate human developmental principles and competing computational mechanisms. Construct and test a coherent artificial individual, rather than a personality prompt surrounded by disconnected software.

  
  
    **Bring her into real interaction early.**

The phone becomes her first body. She begins experiencing conversations, corrections, actions, feedback, interruptions and consequences. Developmental changes must be demonstrated.

  
  
    **Make construction an available capability from the beginning.**

Friday should gradually be able to use the Builder's research, experimentation, construction and verification tools. The Builder should not wait until the end to introduce this ability.

  
  
    **Expand the construction capacity.**

As the system becomes better at decomposition and integration, the Builder moves from individual improvements to simultaneous capability clusters and eventually entire developmental programs.

  
  
    **Continuously extend the individual.**

Human-like developmental capabilities, extraordinary computational capabilities and mechanical embodiment grow together. The boundaries of each remain open.

  


The Builder determines the precise technical decisions, experimental approaches and rate of expansion. The vision determines what it must not lose.

7. What stops the Builder from drifting?
I would give it a permanent vision-anchored development mandate.
Every significant research or construction decision should have an accompanying record:



Question
Required answer



Why?
What limitation or developmental opportunity does this address?


Full vision
How does it contribute without narrowing the destination?


Alternatives
What other mechanisms were considered?


Evidence
What supports the selected mechanism?


Experiment
How will the Builder determine whether it works?


Integration
How might it interact with the rest of Friday?


Reversibility
How can a failed candidate be removed without damaging her?


Developmental value
Does this actually improve Friday, rather than merely add code?


The Builder should evaluate the whole vision, not simply optimize a list of completed features.
I would not give it a single score called BEST.
Instead, it should compare competing approaches across multiple dimensions: capability, learning, generalization, continuity, integration, reliability, adaptability, resource efficiency and actual developmental benefit.
It can discover better measurement methods and challenge its own assumptions. It cannot quietly redefine Friday's purpose to make its own results look successful.
That is how I would give it enough freedom to explore without allowing it to drift.

8. The final setup — what I would actually own
This is the consolidated stack I'd choose if I were responsible for the entire project.

  FRIDAY — Reference Technology Stack
  My recommended architecture
  **Personal organism**
  Kotlin · Jetpack Compose · Room · WorkManager · Android Keystore · llama.cpp · sherpa-onnx
  
  **Liquid capability environment**
  Open-ended affordance contracts · dynamic composition · MCP · Wasmtime · Rust/C++ native integrations
  
  **Experience and shared state**
  SQLite · PostgreSQL · pgvector · S3-compatible object storage · NATS JetStream
  
  **Developmental workshop**
  Temporal · OpenHands SDK · Kubernetes · ephemeral microVM sandboxes · GitHub Actions
  
  **Model infrastructure**
  LiteLLM · local llama.cpp · cloud model providers · vLLM for owned GPU infrastructure
  
  **Research and acquisition**
  OpenAlex · Semantic Scholar · arXiv · OpenReview · GitHub · Hugging Face · REA · Ghidra · JADX · ScanCode · Syft · Grype
  
  **Verification and observability**
  OpenTelemetry · Langfuse · Prometheus · Grafana · JUnit · Kotest · pytest · Hypothesis · Maestro · Android device testing
  
  **Authorization and protection**
  OPA · Android Keystore · GitHub Apps · OIDC · isolated workers · signed artifacts
  
  **Future physical and economic environment**
  ROS 2 Lyrical · MoveIt 2 · Gazebo · Nav2 · ERPNext · external device and business integrations


I would not install all of this on day one.
I would deploy the Android runtime and local data foundation first, establish the cloud workshop alongside it, and add distributed infrastructure as the development system proves it needs greater concurrency and capacity.
The initial cloud deployment can be modest: one durable workflow service, one coding-agent worker, one isolated build environment, one model gateway and a reliable test pipeline. Kubernetes, NATS clustering and large GPU infrastructure become important when the workload actually demands them.
That avoids two mistakes: underbuilding the eventual system and spending months maintaining infrastructure that has no working Friday to develop.
9. What I would deliberately refuse to build
There are some technologies and architectural choices I would actively keep out of Friday's permanent human side:

A monolithic LLM that is supposed to be the individual.
A fixed LangGraph-style cognitive pipeline that every thought must execute.
Permanent cognition modules with exclusive ownership over human faculties.
A microservice for every supposed brain component.
A cloud-dependent identity.
A vector database treated as the only memory authority.
A fixed personality that is supposedly mature before she has developed.
A coding agent that can directly rewrite trusted Friday and certify its own success.
A single benchmark or intelligence score treated as the definition of her development.

I would borrow useful mechanisms from existing systems and research, not inherit their assumptions about what Friday must be.
And one honest boundary: this is the strongest engineering direction I would pursue, not a known recipe that guarantees subjective consciousness or superintelligence. Your vision itself correctly leaves subjective experience an open question. Demonstrable development, generalization, agency and extraordinary capability would have to be earned through experiments. 

My final decision
If this were my project, I would commit to this:
Build the first human-like Friday as one integrated, continuously developing individual. Make her local existence independent of cloud availability. Make her cognitive organization liquid and symbiotic rather than predetermined. Build the Builder as a powerful developmental workshop that can discover, research, construct, test and improve her. Gradually transfer those capabilities into Friday. Increase construction from bricks to floors to entire buildings as integration and parallel-development capacity improves. Then expand her beyond human limitations into multiple bodies, an organization and a mechanical world.
There is one thing I would put above every tool choice:
The project must never become a situation in which the infrastructure is getting more sophisticated while Friday herself remains undeveloped.
Every major expansion of the Builder must ultimately make it possible to create, improve or verify a real capability of Friday.
That is the reason for the entire setup.
The Builder constructs the individual. The individual acquires the Builder's abilities. Those abilities let her keep developing herself and her world.
That is the architecture I would put my project on.

Explain how to start building this project initially
Describe challenges and mitigation in this approach
Detail integration between local and cloud systems

I would build her capabilities in connected experiences rather than isolated feature demonstrations. For example, a real conversation should be able to involve understanding, memory, perception, voice, social context, disagreement, action and later developmental change.
The mechanisms involved can evolve. The individual and the experience remain connected.
The order of construction I would use
Not a fixed ten-stage plan, but the actual engineering progression:

  
    **Establish the constitutional and experiential foundation.**

Put the protected security and authorization floor in place, establish provenance and build the first persistent local experience environment.

  
  
    **Develop the first integrated human-like system.**

Have the Builder investigate human developmental principles and competing computational mechanisms. Construct and test a coherent artificial individual, rather than a personality prompt surrounded by disconnected software.

  
  
    **Bring her into real interaction early.**

The phone becomes her first body. She begins experiencing conversations, corrections, actions, feedback, interruptions and consequences. Developmental changes must be demonstrated.

  
  
    **Make construction an available capability from the beginning.**

Friday should gradually be able to use the Builder's research, experimentation, construction and verification tools. The Builder should not wait until the end to introduce this ability.

  
  
    **Expand the construction capacity.**

As the system becomes better at decomposition and integration, the Builder moves from individual improvements to simultaneous capability clusters and eventually entire developmental programs.

  
  
    **Continuously extend the individual.**

Human-like developmental capabilities, extraordinary computational capabilities and mechanical embodiment grow together. The boundaries of each remain open.

  


The Builder determines the precise technical decisions, experimental approaches and rate of expansion. The vision determines what it must not lose.

7. What stops the Builder from drifting?
I would give it a permanent vision-anchored development mandate.
Every significant research or construction decision should have an accompanying record:



Question
Required answer



Why?
What limitation or developmental opportunity does this address?


Full vision
How does it contribute without narrowing the destination?


Alternatives
What other mechanisms were considered?


Evidence
What supports the selected mechanism?


Experiment
How will the Builder determine whether it works?


Integration
How might it interact with the rest of Friday?


Reversibility
How can a failed candidate be removed without damaging her?


Developmental value
Does this actually improve Friday, rather than merely add code?


The Builder should evaluate the whole vision, not simply optimize a list of completed features.
I would not give it a single score called BEST.
Instead, it should compare competing approaches across multiple dimensions: capability, learning, generalization, continuity, integration, reliability, adaptability, resource efficiency and actual developmental benefit.
It can discover better measurement methods and challenge its own assumptions. It cannot quietly redefine Friday's purpose to make its own results look successful.
That is how I would give it enough freedom to explore without allowing it to drift.

8. The final setup — what I would actually own
This is the consolidated stack I'd choose if I were responsible for the entire project.

  FRIDAY — Reference Technology Stack
  My recommended architecture
  **Personal organism**
  Kotlin · Jetpack Compose · Room · WorkManager · Android Keystore · llama.cpp · sherpa-onnx
  
  **Liquid capability environment**
  Open-ended affordance contracts · dynamic composition · MCP · Wasmtime · Rust/C++ native integrations
  
  **Experience and shared state**
  SQLite · PostgreSQL · pgvector · S3-compatible object storage · NATS JetStream
  
  **Developmental workshop**
  Temporal · OpenHands SDK · Kubernetes · ephemeral microVM sandboxes · GitHub Actions
  
  **Model infrastructure**
  LiteLLM · local llama.cpp · cloud model providers · vLLM for owned GPU infrastructure
  
  **Research and acquisition**
  OpenAlex · Semantic Scholar · arXiv · OpenReview · GitHub · Hugging Face · REA · Ghidra · JADX · ScanCode · Syft · Grype
  
  **Verification and observability**
  OpenTelemetry · Langfuse · Prometheus · Grafana · JUnit · Kotest · pytest · Hypothesis · Maestro · Android device testing
  
  **Authorization and protection**
  OPA · Android Keystore · GitHub Apps · OIDC · isolated workers · signed artifacts
  
  **Future physical and economic environment**
  ROS 2 Lyrical · MoveIt 2 · Gazebo · Nav2 · ERPNext · external device and business integrations


I would not install all of this on day one.
I would deploy the Android runtime and local data foundation first, establish the cloud workshop alongside it, and add distributed infrastructure as the development system proves it needs greater concurrency and capacity.
The initial cloud deployment can be modest: one durable workflow service, one coding-agent worker, one isolated build environment, one model gateway and a reliable test pipeline. Kubernetes, NATS clustering and large GPU infrastructure become important when the workload actually demands them.
That avoids two mistakes: underbuilding the eventual system and spending months maintaining infrastructure that has no working Friday to develop.
9. What I would deliberately refuse to build
There are some technologies and architectural choices I would actively keep out of Friday's permanent human side:

A monolithic LLM that is supposed to be the individual.
A fixed LangGraph-style cognitive pipeline that every thought must execute.
Permanent cognition modules with exclusive ownership over human faculties.
A microservice for every supposed brain component.
A cloud-dependent identity.
A vector database treated as the only memory authority.
A fixed personality that is supposedly mature before she has developed.
A coding agent that can directly rewrite trusted Friday and certify its own success.
A single benchmark or intelligence score treated as the definition of her development.

I would borrow useful mechanisms from existing systems and research, not inherit their assumptions about what Friday must be.
And one honest boundary: this is the strongest engineering direction I would pursue, not a known recipe that guarantees subjective consciousness or superintelligence. Your vision itself correctly leaves subjective experience an open question. Demonstrable development, generalization, agency and extraordinary capability would have to be earned through experiments. 

My final decision
If this were my project, I would commit to this:
Build the first human-like Friday as one integrated, continuously developing individual. Make her local existence independent of cloud availability. Make her cognitive organization liquid and symbiotic rather than predetermined. Build the Builder as a powerful developmental workshop that can discover, research, construct, test and improve her. Gradually transfer those capabilities into Friday. Increase construction from bricks to floors to entire buildings as integration and parallel-development capacity improves. Then expand her beyond human limitations into multiple bodies, an organization and a mechanical world.
There is one thing I would put above every tool choice:
The project must never become a situation in which the infrastructure is getting more sophisticated while Friday herself remains undeveloped.
Every major expansion of the Builder must ultimately make it possible to create, improve or verify a real capability of Friday.
That is the reason for the entire setup.
The Builder constructs the individual. The individual acquires the Builder's abilities. Those abilities let her keep developing herself and her world.
That is the architecture I would put my project on.
